Stone-Zsela separately would affirm under a narrower proposed extension of Rauscher: the United States may not use its agents to seize a Mexican national from Mexico over formal objection and thereby defeat Article9’s retained surrender decision. He acknowledges textual silence and confines the inference to that governmental bypass. He preserves Ker–Frisbie outside this boundary and would openly narrow Ker if it otherwise covered it. Return restores the pre-abduction position; dismissal is no acquittal or permanent bar after lawful transfer. He reserves private captures without attribution, consensual transfers, future extradition, and damages, and reaches no independent constitutional or international-law capture exception.

- **Ankenbrandt v. Richards (1992-06-15):** Stone-Zsela concurs in the judgment because §1332 reaches this independent tort action even assuming that a traditional decree-based exception exists. He reserves whether that exception should ultimately survive and its full foundation and reach. The requested remedy controls: ordinary tort adjudication and compensation neither create a federal substantive claim nor modify family status. He rejects Younger because no proceeding is ongoing and Burford because no necessary status question or demonstrated interference appears; he reserves materially changed facts and the truth of the allegations. Blackmun also concurs in the judgment, rejects a categorical statutory exception, and would address the institutional concerns through appropriately confined abstention. Stevens, joined by Thomas, agrees that the case is plainly within diversity jurisdiction and that dismissal is unjustified, while reserving the unnecessary foundational question whether the exception exists or what principle supports it. None of these reservations subtracts from the five votes supporting the Court’s limited statutory rule.

- **Morgan v. Illinois (1992-06-15):** Stone-Zsela concurs in the judgment. On request, inquiry must adequately identify automatic-death views that prevent or substantially impair lawful consideration of mitigation. General assurances here did not do so, and neither presumed favorable answers nor the strength of the guilt evidence supplies a lawful sentencing tribunal. He preserves Witt, trial-court control of wording and follow-up, and juror eligibility despite general support for death or a view that some proved circumstances strongly favor it. He finds no actual juror bias and reserves unrelated omissions and collateral-review questions. His remand position requires a properly selected jury for any renewed capital sentencing, while allowing Illinois to impose a lawful noncapital sentence. Scalia, joined by Thomas, dissents on two sufficient grounds: a juror may consider mitigation yet regard it as insufficient without violating the actual instructions; and, even assuming categorical disqualification, the extensive general inquiry satisfies Mu’Min and warrants deference. They distinguish a refusal to obey the law from a strict but legally permissible assessment of punishment and would affirm the sentence.

- **Patterson v. Shumate (1992-06-15):** Scalia joins the entire Court opinion and emphasizes consistent usage within the Bankruptcy Code. His criticism of the interpretive approach used in Dewsnup does not qualify the present holding or overrule that decision. Stone concurs in the judgment on the enacted federal-law-inclusive phrase and the established trust and enforceability predicates. Section 522 independently protects interests not already excluded, and Guidry prevents equitable disapproval of the beneficiary from overriding the enacted antialienation protection. His separate writing reserves plans outside ERISA, nontrust interests, statutory exceptions and distributed benefits; it supplies no general pension immunity or constitutional pension entitlement.

- **Allied-Signal, Inc. v. Director, Division of Taxation (1992-06-15):** Stone concurs in the judgment. He retains the territorial/unitary limit, permits genuine operational investments without unity between issuer and holder, and finds no identifiable operational function for the ASARCO interest. Later use of proceeds alone is insufficient; calculation and appropriate refund treatment remain below. New Jersey has shown no collapse of the received territorial rule's premise or practical unworkability. Concrete multistate tax and investment reliance supports continuity, and disagreement over fact-sensitive applications does not justify abandoning the boundary. He reserves genuine operational investments and fair-apportionment disputes on a proper tax base. Corporate status neither defeats constitutional tax limits nor creates immunity from state taxation.

O'Connor, joined by Blackmun and Thomas, would affirm. They agree that common ownership is insufficient and that an operational investment can qualify without a unitary issuer and holder. They conclude that Bendix failed to prove the gain unrelated to its unitary aerospace business because intended deployment toward the Martin Marietta acquisition could establish the required operational link. They reject a constitutional distinction resting merely on investment duration and would defer to the state court's record-supported conclusion. Those propositions are noncontrolling where they differ from the Court's application.

#### Chunk 12 published separate positions

- **Nordlinger v. Hahn, 1992-06-18:** Thomas, concurrence in part and judgment, June 18: conceivable rationality supports the scheme, but the Court’s distinction of Allegheny Pittsburgh is unpersuasive and risks confusing state-law error with equal protection; noncontrolling criticism, not an overruling. Stevens, dissent, June 18: rationality still requires an impartial purpose and meaningful fit; pervasive, inheritable advantages cannot justify themselves by their own continuation; would reverse, no travel holding. Stone-Zsela, judgment concurrence, June 18: predictable ownership commitments and the specified family/older-owner policies suffice here; state-law deviations, a proper traveler and independent statutory tax claims remain reserved. All are published noncontrolling explanations except the Court holdings independently supported above.

- **Georgia v. McCollum, 1992-06-18:** Thomas, judgment concurrence, June 18: follows Edmonson as governing law despite a first-impression objection to state attribution; criticizes regulating peremptories for limiting defendants’ protection against concealed bias. His criticism is noncontrolling. O’Connor, dissent, June 18: criminal defense’s constitutionally independent adversarial role distinguishes civil selection and defeats fair attribution under Polk County; minority representation may mitigate concealed bias, but no Court factfinding about this jury follows. Scalia, dissent, June 18: accepts Edmonson’s logical consequence while rejecting its correctness; would not extend it to defense peremptories. Stone-Zsela, judgment concurrence, June 18: act-specific state power, ordinary Powers standing and unchanged Batson purpose/persuasion rules suffice; no general defense state-actor status, concrete privilege ruling, nonracial classification or unconscious-bias doctrine. These separate accounts do not add controlling law beyond the Court holdings.

- **Kraft General Foods, Inc. v. Iowa Department of Revenue and Finance, 1992-06-18:** Blackmun, dissent, June 18: a facial attack requires attention to the scope of foreign activity and total domestic/foreign tax burdens; the record does not establish systematic disadvantage, and federal conformity rationally defeats equal protection. He would affirm; the position is noncontrolling and does not qualify the Court’s source-discrimination rule. Stone-Zsela, judgment concurrence, June 18: favoring all domestic States still disadvantages foreign commerce; partial federal conformity is not permission, convenience is insufficient where neutral adjustment is possible, and speculation about other taxes is not a matched compensating burden. He reserves separate Japan Line theories and calculation; noncontrolling separate reasoning. His fallback nonreach of equal protection adds no published substantive proposition.

- **Gade v. National Solid Wastes Management Association, 1992-06-18:** Souter, joined by Blackmun, Stevens and Thomas, plurality, June 18: traditional state standards may coexist whenever compliance with both is physically possible; §18’s savings and replacement-plan provisions do not support automatic exclusivity. Its positive physical-impossibility-only formulation is noncontrolling beyond the shared negative holding. Stone-Zsela, judgment concurrence, June18: a clearly enacted ceiling or exclusive arrangement and a demonstrated conflict would displace state law; neither overlap nor a dual public purpose resolves that inquiry. This reserved conflict account is not itself a newly adopted majority test. O’Connor, joined by White and Scalia, dissent, June18: §18’s integrated approval, transition and withdrawal scheme implies exclusive regulation of federally covered occupational issues; public purpose and licensing labels do not avoid that result. Kennedy, dissent, June18: statutory text/structure expressly produces that exclusivity, but generalized tensions or hypothetical conflicts are insufficient; no join to the obstacle rationale. Both dissents are noncontrolling.

- **Forsyth County v. Nationalist Movement, 1992-06-19:** Published noncontrolling positions: Stone-Zsela's concurrence, June 19, preserves fixed neutral fees, actual neutral costs, concrete indigency challenges, and rejects viewpoint-dependent entitlement to protection; compatible with, but not a formal join in, the Court opinion. White, Scalia, and Thomas's dissent, June 19, preserves Cox's variable neutral fees and reserves alternative facial grounds for findings rather than authorizing censorship. No external consequence is admitted. All related City/Board claims stay outside this Supreme Court event.

- **New York v. United States, 1992-06-19:** Published noncontrolling positions, all June 19: Stone-Zsela's concurrence treats only a manageable institutional Guarantee claim as justiciable, then rejects it; reserves wider theories, actual compacts, Reconstruction enforcement, state-court federal duties, and direct State market regulation. This is not current controlling justiciability law. White, Blackmun, and Stevens's concurrence/dissent treat the negotiated arrangement, assent and benefits, political safeguards, and cooperative regulation as sufficient to sustain take-title and support estoppel; they dissent from severing it. Their suggestions concerning possible future spending/direct-regulation alternatives do not validate any unpresented replacement statute. Stevens's own writing additionally rejects a categorical ban on congressional state-directed commands and would recognize direct remedial authority in interstate controversies; no other Justice joins that separate writing. No external consequence, new compact, or new case is admitted.

- **Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., 1992-06-19:** The Court opinion changes only the described statutory construction. O’Connor’s protected-freshness view and independently sufficient inventory/delivery ground, Kennedy and Blackmun’s reasonable-buyer significant-value approach, and Stone-Zsela’s business-activity explanation of triviality are published noncontrolling positions at their stated scopes. No roster, allotment, referral practice, retained Supreme Court matter or companion dependency changes.

- **American National Red Cross v. S. G., 1992-06-19:** Souter’s five-Justice opinion supplies the law. The capacity-only interpretation of Scalia, O’Connor and Kennedy is published but noncontrolling, including its refusal to reach ArticleIII. Stone-Zsela’s charter-specific concurrence and reservations are published noncontrolling positions; no broader formal join is inferred. No institutional, roster, participation, circuit, referral or retained-docket change occurs.

- **Wright v. West, 1992-06-19:** Published noncontrolling positions: Thomas and Scalia reserve whether preexisting habeas doctrine permits broader reasonableness review and discuss finality without adopting that standard as law. White endorses only the sufficient-evidence ground. O’Connor, Blackmun and Stevens affirm independent legal review and reject the proposed Teague conversion; Kennedy separately distinguishes new-rule retroactivity from deference and regards genuinely novel applications of a general fact-sensitive standard as unusual without resolving this claim’s novelty. Souter would apply Teague first and treats the specific Cosby-based limitation here as new, taking no position in the historical habeas dispute. Stone-Zsela’s independent Jackson concurrence preserves affirmative-proof, burden and propensity boundaries and reserves genuine extensions and exceptions. These broader separate propositions are noncontrolling at their respective scopes. Simulated Stringer and Keeney retain their exact existing operation. No institutional, roster, allotment, referral or retained-docket change occurs.

- **United States v. Salerno, 1992-06-19:** Blackmun joins the Court and explains that similar does not mean identical. The actual issues and context of questioning govern, with neither a categorical prosecutorial exemption nor automatic admission; he favors remand because the appropriate inquiry was not completed below.

Stone-Zsela concurs in the judgment. He requires the defense to establish the evidentiary conditions without shifting the prosecution's burden to prove guilt beyond a reasonable doubt. The prosecutor may be investigating the same alleged conspiracy or different issues; actual motive governs. Contradictory independent evidence is not disclosure or adoption of the prior account. His disclosure commitments do not convert access into admissibility. He reserves properly preserved independent constitutional objections, other exceptions and the factual motive determination, and preserves independent appellate grounds.

Stevens would affirm because the same crucial question—Cedar Park's participation in the alleged Club—gave the Government a similar motive to test the witnesses' denials. A tactical choice not to use the full examination opportunity does not eliminate that motive. He agrees that genuinely different issues can defeat similarity; his conclusion that it is established here is noncontrolling.

#### Chunk 13 published separate positions

- **Reynolds v. International Amateur Athletic Federation, 1992-06-20:** No explanatory separate writing; noted disagreement creates no published noncontrolling legal proposition.

- **Sawyer v. Whitley, 1992-06-22:** Stevens, Blackmun and O'Connor, Sawyer judgment concurrence, June 22, 1992: a capital prisoner should show more likely than not that the sentence is clearly erroneous, considering all evidence including decisive mitigation and selection as well as eligibility; sentence plausibility on the whole record precludes relief under that demanding inquiry. They reject the clear-and-convincing quantum and the eligibility-only boundary. Noncontrolling; they nonetheless affirm this record. Blackmun's separate concurrence: constitutional fundamental fairness is not exhausted by factual innocence, and restrictions on correction of capital constitutional error threaten fair administration; he announces no categorical invalidation of capital punishment. Stone-Zsela's separate concurrence: more likely than not that no reasonable sentencer would choose death on the corrected record, including eligibility and constitutionally relevant mitigation, is the proposed capital gateway; mere reweighting does not suffice, and Sawyer fails. Cause, exhaustion, retroactivity and merits remain distinct. Noncontrolling; no formal Stevens join.

- **R. A. V. v. City of St. Paul, 1992-06-22:** Scalia,joined by Kennedy,Souter and Thomas,would accept the fighting-words narrowing for purposes of decision but invalidate the ordinance's selection of disfavored subjects and its viewpoint operation. Their rule would permit distinctions resting on the very reason the category is proscribable,genuine secondary effects,incidental application of conduct laws,or selectivity with no realistic censorship risk; they conclude that the ordinance satisfies none of those qualifications. This ground has four votes and is not Court law.

White,joined by Blackmun and O'Connor,and by Stevens except on the categorical account,disputes that extension. He regards the particular harms of racial and similar intimidation as a permissible reason for focused regulation of a genuinely unprotected subset; this does not save an ordinance that actually covers protected speech. Blackmun separately warns that treating every speech category alike can weaken protection for core expression and preserve insufficient room to regulate distinct harms. Stevens,with White and Blackmun joining his criticism of the selection theory,also rejects categorical absolutes in his unjoined discussion and treats content,context and the kind of restriction as relevant. These positions explain why the Court's shared ground is overbreadth and why no broader positive rule is adopted.

- **Medina v. California, 1992-06-22:** Kennedy, White, Scalia and Thomas, Medina principal opinion, June 22, 1992: Patterson's historical/fundamental-fairness inquiry should govern this state criminal procedure; Mathews should not be the general framework. Four-vote method, noncontrolling beyond independently shared narrow rules. O'Connor and Souter, judgment concurrence: Mathews remains useful, historical pedigree is rebuttable, and practical access/cooperation considerations support this burden without generally dispensing with balancing. Their broad method is noncontrolling; their precise burden and presumption support is part of the six-Justice holdings. Blackmun and Stevens, dissent: Pate/Drope adequacy requires the State to bear persuasion after sufficient doubt; proof uncertainty and defendant incapacity make allocation constitutionally material. Stone-Zsela, dissent: State persuasion by preponderance after justified doubt, preserving threshold and Dusky; vacate/remand for retrospective feasibility and retrial upon competence if the old process cannot be reliably cured. No actual incompetence finding, higher quantum, acquittal or indefinite commitment is proposed. The State-burden positions remain noncontrolling.

- **Estate of Cowart v. Nicklos Drilling Co., 1992-06-22:** Blackmun, Stevens and O'Connor, Cowart dissent, June 22, 1992: read entitlement in context to require acknowledgment/payment or adjudication before the prior-approval condition attaches; the retained phrase and new notification rule preserve the administrative distinction. They regard the contrary reading as incompatible with the compensatory scheme and the incentives of employers denying liability, and challenge a selective literal reading across§33(b),(f),(g). They would treat notice as sufficient for Cowart and restore statutory eligibility, without claiming a controlling double-recovery right. Noncontrolling; the majority's vested-entitlement rule governs.

- **Cipollone v. Liggett Group, Inc., 1992-06-24:** The public disposition ends Supreme Court review and creates no retained docket or roster/allotment/practice change. Public noncontrolling positions, June24: Blackmun, joined by Kennedy and Souter, would exclude all common-law damages from both cigarette clauses; the broader amended-Act proposition remains noncontrolling. Scalia, joined by Thomas only for express statutory construction and applications, would give the original Act broader warning coverage and the amended Act broader warranty/fraud and practically compelled consumer-warning coverage; these extensions remain noncontrolling. Scalia alone preserves a broader field/obstacle-preemption account; Thomas's partial join preserves his already published Gade position. Stone's compatible statutory joins and actual-impossibility reservation do not establish a freestanding new Court test. All post-event law is confined to the holding blocks. No directly connected later external consequence is admitted; cutoff June24,1992.

- **City of Burlington v. Dague, 1992-06-24:** The enhancement event ends Supreme Court review and creates no retained docket, roster/allotment or institutional change. Published noncontrolling positions, June24: Blackmun and Stevens's dissent accepts this supported contingent-payment adjustment and would affirm; O'Connor would permit the proven market-class differential and remand for a missing amount finding; Stone's dissent requires that differential, substantial access difficulty, mitigation, nonduplication and exclusion of case-specific risk, with no fixed percentage or mandatory enhancement. Each remains noncontrolling. No later external consequence is admitted; cutoff June24,1992.

- **Lee v. Weisman, 1992-06-24:** Souter, joined by Stevens and O'Connor, concurrence, June 24, 1992: beyond the controlling school-sponsorship ground, the Establishment Clause generally forbids endorsement of religion over nonreligion as well as sectarian preferences; coercion is not a universally necessary element. His history supports retaining that received neutrality account. Noncontrolling at that broader level.

Souter, joined by Stevens and O'Connor, concurrence, June 24, 1992: permissible accommodation must lift a discernible burden on religious exercise; mere desire for official affirmation does not itself establish such a burden. Equal access, genuinely private speaker choice and private graduation observance remain distinct. This wider accommodation criterion is noncontrolling here.

Scalia, joined by White and Thomas, dissent, June 24, 1992: law-backed compulsion supplies the relevant coercion boundary; traditional nonsectarian prayer at a voluntary civic ceremony can be accommodated, and respectful silence is not religious assent. The dissent acknowledges sectarian endorsement as a distinct concern and would not authorize penalties for abstention. Its critique of Lemon and indirect-coercion analysis is noncontrolling.

- **Doggett v. United States, 1992-06-24:** O'Connor, dissent, June 24, 1992: on this record the Barker balance does not establish a violation because no actual defense impairment, detention or anxiety is shown and delay can also weaken the prosecution. Her Loud Hawk-based objection to allowing speculative impairment to carry the balance is noncontrolling.

Thomas, joined by Scalia, dissent, June 24, 1992: Barker's inquiry presupposes accusation-related liberty impairment; the Clause does not independently protect an entirely unaware, unrestricted accused against defense erosion or a later disruption of settled life. Limitations statutes and due process, not a constitutional right to repose, address those distinct interests. Noncontrolling; both accept the absence-of-knowledge finding rather than invent evasion.

#### Chunk 14 published separate positions

- **International Society for Krishna Consciousness, Inc. v. Lee, 1992-06-26:** White, O'Connor, Scalia, Thomas: White plurality, June 26, 1992—these terminals are nonpublic and the limited immediate-collection ban is reasonable; four-Justice noncontrolling rationale. Stone: separate concurrence—public classification assumed, Ward satisfied only by immediate-payment restriction with interior later-support advocacy an essential alternative; no classification join, noncontrolling. Kennedy: Part I, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, Souter—objective character/use/compatibility supports public-forum status here; noncontrolling. Kennedy Part II alone—Ward or regulation of the nonspeech transaction element supports the limited collection rule; literature sales warrant different treatment because their exchange and practical alternatives differ, and a flat sales prohibition would fail; these propositions are noncontrolling and establish no majority sales privilege. O'Connor: separate writing—reasonableness must take actual multipurpose use seriously, collection and leafletting differ; noncontrolling application. Souter, Blackmun, Stevens: dissent—complete interior solicitation exclusion fails adequate fit and alternatives; noncontrolling. None changes the law entering an uncoordinated same-day case.

- **United States v. Fordice; Ayers v. Fordice, 1992-06-26:** O'Connor, June 26 concurrence, joins Court: claimed educational justifications require close examination; a genuinely essential remnant's segregative impact should still be counteracted/minimized as far as possible. The added formulation is noncontrolling. Thomas, June 26 concurrence, joins Court: origins, effect and absent justification support a continuing-intent inference; historically black institutions' independent traditions and educational value can be sound reasons for their lawful continuation; no destruction or permanent racial-identifiability entitlement. His distinct intent formulation is noncontrolling. Scalia, June 26 concurrence/dissent: nondiscriminatory access suffices in voluntary universities under his Bazemore reading; demands proof that discriminatory ACT purpose/effect ended, objects to systemic effects review and supports lawful voluntary institutional diversity; no general equal-funding entitlement. His admissions-only disposition is not support for the Court's broader rule.

- **Two Pesos, Inc. v. Taco Cabana, Inc., 1992-06-26:** White's seven-Justice opinion controls. Stevens's judgment concurrence (June 26) grounds the result in developed judicial construction and congressional endorsement, rather than original-text completeness. Thomas's judgment concurrence grounds it in §43(a)'s language and common-law source-identification presumptions rather than registration provisions. Scalia's concurrence adopts Thomas's explanation as complementary while joining the Court. All three separate propositions are noncontrolling at that methodological level; no contrary substantive rule is adopted.

- **Franklin v. Massachusetts, 1992-06-26:** O'Connor, joined by White, Scalia and Thomas, views the Secretary's report as an intermediate step because the President transmits the operative apportionment and may seek corrections; they would also exclude the President from the APA's agency definition. They concur in the constitutional rule and reversal but oppose review of the Secretary under §704. Scalia additionally explains that this Court's express Montana standing decision, which he joined, governs likely declaratory redress for the proved inclusion injury; it does not decide whether a court may coerce the President. These positions do not displace the five-Justice APA holdings.

- **Lee v. International Society for Krishna Consciousness, Inc., 1992-06-26:** Stone: June 26 concurrence—assumed nonpublic status, actual multipurpose compatibility, and no demonstrated reasonable basis for total leafletting exclusion; no stricter least-restrictive-means requirement; noncontrolling. O'Connor: companion writing—actual nonpublic classification with contextual unreasonableness; noncontrolling. Kennedy, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter: Kennedy Part I—public-forum classification and invalidity under Ward; noncontrolling. Souter, Blackmun, Stevens also preserve their solicitation dissent in the companion. Kennedy's sole Part II sales distinction remains a published noncontrolling position as recorded in No. 91-155; it supplies no majority sales right. White, Scalia, Thomas: distribution dissent—managerial cumulative congestion, litter and monitoring grounds suffice under nonpublic reasonableness; noncontrolling. No new roster, institutional or allotment change; the interparty injunction continues without retained Supreme Court supervision.

- **Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 1992-06-29:** All positions below are published in Casey on June 29, 1992 and are noncontrolling except for exact propositions separately identified in Holdings.

- O’Connor, Kennedy and Souter, joint Part III: replace the general trimester scheme with a purpose-or-effect substantial-obstacle rule for previability regulation; an identified obstacle is unconstitutional without a second compelling-interest rescue. Legitimate health/potential-life regulation and truthful persuasion may operate absent such an obstacle. This general formula has three joins; its issue-specific judgment-supporting applications have the narrower force identified above.
- Stone, separate writing: retain the received Roe/Doe/Akron/Thornburgh framework; protected bodily and medical choice does not require the activity to be named in the Constitution; changing membership and disputed trimester criticisms do not justify withdrawal on this record. Invalidate mandatory delay, state persuasion and physician exclusivity lacking demonstrated medical need; retain accurate relevant medical and gestational-age information. A minor’s basic independent bypass is permissible, but invalid counseling cannot return through parental consent. Grave, medically established mental-health danger remains relevant to the received health safeguard; no individual denied-treatment finding or bare-preference exception is made. His notice reasoning uses Danforth and the record without adopting a numerical threshold. His report approval excludes patient exposure and materially different burdens.
- Blackmun, separate writing: Roe’s received fundamental-right protection and strict scrutiny remain proper; physician exclusivity, persuasion, waiting and actual informed-parent burdens fail, while ordinary medical content is permissible. The particular physician-judgment/referral reports and public institutional disclosures do not have the demonstrated compelling health justification required by that approach; confidentiality alone does not resolve the burden. Bodily integrity and the constitutional objection to enforced roles reinforce his liberty analysis without creating a new equality holding.
- Stevens, separate writing: the State may offer its own information but may not compel the adult’s reconsideration through the prescribed persuasion or unsupported waiting period; ordinary medical risk information differs from the compulsory gestational-age and alternatives program as imposed here. Basic one-parent consent with an effective bypass is acceptable; imported invalid instructions are not. His approval of reports is limited to the protected-identity and no-proved-obstacle record.
- White, joined by Scalia and Thomas: a liberty interest in abortion is not a fundamental constitutional abortion right; rational review permits all challenged rules and Salerno defeats the facial notice claim. State interests in family communication and potential life distinguish legal notice from a spouse’s legal veto.
- Scalia, joined by White and Thomas: the constitutional text and relevant legal tradition do not establish a special abortion right; Roe should be expressly overruled, and the policy balance left to ordinary legislation subject to applicable constitutional constraints. The general undue-burden formulation is insufficiently determinate; institutional legitimacy does not warrant insulating a constitutional error because it is controversial.

No roster, circuit allotment or standing practice changes. Both merits dockets terminate here subject to remand; lower proceedings remain open for conformity and severability. No same-day independent Lucas or other June 29 holding enters this decision; no later Benten event enters. No external consequence is admitted by this record.

- **Lucas v. South Carolina Coastal Council, 1992-06-29:** Kennedy agrees that the earlier interval is reviewable and reversal appropriate, but would test complete-loss restrictions against objectively reasonable investment-backed expectations drawn from the whole legal tradition. Common-law nuisance alone is too narrow, particularly for vulnerable coastal land; actual ability and intent to develop and the duration of loss remain for remand. Blackmun disputes finality and the unsupported premise of complete economic extinction, and rejects the categorical rule in favor of the protective public purpose and contextual precedent. Stevens would withhold premature constitutional adjudication and, on the merits, sustain the general coastal measure under Penn Central and Keystone; he questions the zero-value boundary and a rigid nuisance exception. Souter would dismiss because the unreviewable and questionable total-loss premise prevents sound resolution of both the category and its exceptions. Each position is noncontrolling.

- **Espinosa v. Florida, 1992-06-29:** White would grant the petition and obtain oral argument before deciding the merits; his procedural dissent does not state a merits position on the preserved claim. Scalia would deny review because the valid aggravators already satisfy the constitutionally required narrowing of the death-eligible class. Following his Walton and Sochor position, he rejects the premise that the Eighth Amendment separately requires removal of an invalid factor from the later weighing stage. That substantive view has one vote and does not control.

- **Benten v. Kessler, 1992-07-17:** Stone-Zsela concurs in the Court's remedy-based denial. He regards the imminent injury as serious and would excuse a judicial exhaustion requirement sufficiently to examine the procedural claim. Automatic detention removed prior field discretion, and the version of §10.40(d) in force in 1989 required additional process even if the change were interpretive or procedural. He finds no qualifying published notice or explained good-cause invocation in this record. Accardi and Nixon require an agency to follow its binding rules; Vermont Yankee limits judicial invention of extra procedures but does not erase procedures the agency adopted. This substantial challenge cannot be answered by citing the narrower rule effective in 1991. He nevertheless finds insufficient authority to convert restored discretion into mandatory admission, reserves final procedural merits and §10.70, accepts the individual medical findings, and decides no constitutional claim.

Blackmun would grant the application; no further public reason is supplied. Stevens would grant because, on this individual record, the asserted federal interest does not justify the burden on Benten's protected choice and her noninvasive method. He regards the absence of a demonstrated significant health risk under the personal-use policy as central. His constitutional analysis is a dissent, not a determination of the claim by the Court.

### 5. Current Procedure and Institution

- **Roster / seniority:** Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. Six qualified Justices constitute a quorum; five votes supply a majority for the nine participating Justices in every completed chunk 6 event; earlier reduced-Court thresholds remain as recorded.
- **Circuit allotments:** Stone—D.C., Fourth, Federal; Souter—First; Scalia—Second and Third; O'Connor—Fifth; Stevens—Sixth and Seventh; Blackmun—Eighth; Kennedy—Ninth; White—Tenth; Thomas—Eleventh. Unchanged from the October 23 institutional record.
- **Participation:** The pre-oath argument rule remains. Thomas did not participate in chunk 4's Doherty, Rufo/Rapone or Dewsnup; all nine participated in White, Wyoming, Elias-Zacarias, Presley/Mack and Lechmere. All nine participated at argument and decision in every chunk 5 event; each was argued after Thomas’s oath. Earlier participation facts remain in entries 1–25. All nine participated at argument and decision in each completed chunk 6 event; all arguments were after Thomas’s oath.
- **Open Supreme Court proceeding:** *Wyoming v. Oklahoma*, No. 112, Original. The January 22 declaration and injunction operate now; jurisdiction is retained for implementation. No later decree, filing, hearing, compliance finding or terminal event is established. The next nonroutine act would be a supported implementation request, if filed. No exact date may be inferred, and the proceeding must be expressly terminated or carried forward before it is removed from live matters.
- **Other stages:** Supreme Court merits review has ended in the other seven chunk 4 events. Doherty returns for implementation of asylum reversal and the surviving withholding judgment; Rufo returns for a new Rule 60(b)(5) assessment with its decree still binding; Elias-Zacarias returns for implementation of the five-Justice disposition sustaining the Board's denial. Presley leaves other district-court claims and the unappealed Road Supervision ruling intact. Earlier lower-court remands remain on their stated terms; none implies retained Supreme Court supervision.
- **Chunk 5 stages:** Supreme Court merits review ends in all nine events. Hudson returns without the rejected injury floor or a new award; Nordic Village’s monetary judgment is reversed; Holywell’s ordinary remand applies the specified filing/payment duties; Franklin returns for liability, attribution, causation and loss; INDOPCO’s denial of immediate deductions stands; Arkansas’s permit is sustained with existing conditions; Collins’s federal dismissal stands; Willy’s $19,307 sanction and prior state-court direction stand; McCarthy returns without the rejected exhaustion gate. No new Supreme Court proceeding, return date or external follow-on act is established.

- **Chunk 6 stages:** Dawson v. Delaware: Supreme Court review of the sentence ends; the case returns to Delaware for further proceedings. No resentencing date, harmlessness finding, stay, or retained Supreme Court supervision is created. The convictions remain intact. General Motors Corp. v. Romein: Supreme Court review ends with affirmance. Michigan’s repayment obligation may be enforced on its existing terms; the Court orders no additional award, contract modification, or retained supervision. Williams v. United States: Supreme Court sentence review ends with remand to the Seventh Circuit. That court must decide whether the same sentence would have been imposed and whether the departure is independently reasonable; if either required condition fails, it must remand for resentencing. No new sentence or automatic district-court hearing is imposed now. Stringer v. Black: Supreme Court review ends with remand to the Fifth Circuit. The preserved procedural-default defense remains undecided; the lower courts must consider it and any adequate state correction. If relief is otherwise warranted, a conditional writ may permit resentencing or authorized appellate cure. No immediate release, automatic life sentence or finding that a particular state cure failed is made. Connecticut National Bank v. Germain: Supreme Court review of the jurisdictional dismissal ends. The case returns to the Second Circuit to consider §1292’s applicable requirements and whether to permit the appeal. The underlying jury dispute remains undecided here. PFZ Properties, Inc. v. Rodriguez: The writ is dismissed; Supreme Court review ends. The First Circuit’s judgment remains in force without an affirmance on the merits. No permit, damages, new hearing, retained supervision or reopening of excluded claims is ordered. United States v. R. L. C.: Supreme Court review ends in affirmance of the Eighth Circuit. The district court’s already-entered 18-month disposition is not replaced by a new remand order. No additional detention or new departure finding is made. The Government’s effort to restore the three-year approach fails. Nationwide Mutual Insurance Co. v. Darden: Supreme Court review ends with remand to the Fourth Circuit for the proper classification inquiry and further proceedings consistent with that result. Neither Darden’s employee status nor an entitlement to benefits is finally determined. The excluded cross-petition issue is not reopened. United States v. Wilson: Supreme Court review ends in reversal of the circuit’s direction to award credit at sentencing. BOP remains responsible for the initial calculation after commencement, with administrative and judicial review through proper channels. The Court awards no days of credit and makes no new detention order. Holmes is completed by correction: its two SIPC claim components return for judgment for Holmes; distinct trustee claims and lawful estate recovery remain, with no new return to this Court. No new external filing, return date or retained Supreme Court proceeding is inferred.

- **Chunk 7 participation:** Nine participate at argument/submission and decision in Suter, Felix, Barnhill, Amtrak, Robertson, Commerce v. Montana, Yee, Jacobson and Trevino. Freeman has eight at both stages; Thomas took no part, its argument preceded his oath, and no reargument is established. Five joins are required for a controlling merits proposition in each completed case. Trevino’s public action reports the grant without a petition poll.

- **Chunk 7 stages:** United States v. Felix: The Tenth Circuit must give effect to the reversal as to counts 1–6 and resolve any preserved outstanding appellate matters. No new evidentiary ruling or automatic new trial is ordered. No subsequent filing, mandate-issuance date or rehearing is established. Barnhill v. Johnson: The honor-date ruling stands. Further administration of the preference claim remains subject to its other elements and available defenses; no additional factual finding or remedial order is invented. No subsequent event is established. National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Boston & Maine Corp.: Both cases return to the D.C. Circuit for the compensation challenges and other outstanding review issues. The Court does not itself fix a new valuation, resolve distinct regulatory claims, or schedule further Supreme Court proceedings. No later filing is established. Robertson v. Seattle Audubon Society: The courts apply the new criteria to the still-live sale disputes. Fiscal-year limits and continued application to covered sales contracts remain. No new sale approval, later filing or Supreme Court date is supplied. Freeman v. Pitts: The Eleventh Circuit identifies issues properly open under the parties’ earlier presentations and orders the necessary proceedings, including district-court findings on good faith and remedial connections. Any withdrawal order must define released obligations and remaining supervision. Existing factual findings receive ordinary clear-error review; no general retrial of settled facts is ordered. No new remedy, final discharge, filing date or Supreme Court supervision is invented. Yee v. City of Escondido: The California judgment stands on the physical-taking theory. Any properly available regulatory or as-applied claim must proceed in an appropriate case under its own procedural requirements. There is no new merits remand, withdrawal denial, compensation order or later filing. Jacobson v. United States: The case returns through the Eighth Circuit for entry of acquittal, with no second trial on this charge to repair evidentiary insufficiency. No separate outrageous-conduct ruling, amendment to the offense, new investigation prerequisite or later procedural event is created. Trevino v. Texas: The state court must consider the preserved racial-strike claim, obtaining explanations and findings as appropriate under Batson. Preservation alone orders no new trial. The Court does not decide the consequences of an inability to complete a future corrective inquiry. Other trial errors are not decided here. No execution date, stay, completed hearing or later filing is invented.

The jurisdictional dismissal does not resolve the statute's geographic reach or preclude a later action supported by sufficient standing facts. No mandate-issuance date, rehearing, later proceeding or retained Supreme Court matter is established.

- **Republic of Argentina v. Weltover, Inc. (1992-06-12):** Supreme Court merits review is complete. The Second Circuit's judgment stands, and the district contract action proceeds subject to undecided defenses and ordinary procedure. The Court orders no damages or relief against assets, and establishes no mandate-issuance date, subsequent filing, later judgment or retained Supreme Court proceeding.

- **FTC v. Ticor Title Insurance Co. (1992-06-12):** Supreme Court merits review ends. This Court does not automatically reinstate the entire Commission order, set rates, award damages, direct a new regulatory form or decide the underdeveloped separation-of-powers claim. No later finding, remand result, mandate-issuance date, rehearing or retained Supreme Court proceeding is established.

- **Burlington Northern Railroad Co. v. Ford (1992-06-12):** Supreme Court venue review ends. The Court orders no Hill County transfer, new venue factfinding, damages, negligence determination or special federal-forum election. Other preserved trial defenses remain available on their own terms. No mandate-issuance date, later trial result, new filing, rehearing or retained Supreme Court proceeding is established.

- **United States v. Alvarez-Machain (1992-06-15):** Supreme Court merits review of the Treaty ground is complete. The Ninth Circuit judgment is reversed, and the case is remanded for further proceedings with the treaty-based dismissal and repatriation barrier removed. - **Morgan v. Illinois (1992-06-15):** Supreme Court merits review is complete; the death sentence is set aside and the case returns to Illinois for lawful further sentencing proceedings. The conviction remains intact. No new guilt trial, automatic life sentence, or collateral-review decision is ordered. A future capital jury must be selected consistently with the Court’s inquiry rule.

- **Patterson v. Shumate (1992-06-15):** The merits decision is terminal in this Court. Ordinary issuance of the mandate leaves the Fourth Circuit judgment in effect; no continuing Supreme Court docket, special stay, retained jurisdiction or new institutional practice is created.

On ordinary mandate, the state courts implement the required exclusion and determine calculations and appropriate refund treatment. No other lower tax component is decided here, no special timing order issues, and no retained Supreme Court jurisdiction or institutional change is created.

## Current-Term Neutral Projection

### Projection boundary

The complete Term Continuity Note §§3–5 supplies the substantive public handoff, together with the unchanged opening trackers and public manifest dependencies. Private Stone material, provisional commitments, comparator reconciliation and audit annexes are excluded.

### Authority and chronology

The October 7 opening baseline and 125 effective records reconstruct current state through July 17, 1992, after Benten. The five June26 events precede the common June29 baseline, and all three June29 events precede Benten. The airport companions are coordinated at the same release; no artificial earlier-law status is assigned. Other same-day events are independent. Earlier corrections and the ordered Harris sequence retain their force.

### Current law and public positions

Note §3 preserves every effective controlling rule, authority, date, limit and precedent treatment. Note §4 preserves published noncontrolling positions. Judgment support in the airport cases does not itself supply a majority forum rationale. Franklin makes review of the completed Secretary census decision controlling while reserving presidential APA status and coercive relief. Casey must be read at its exact component and proposition coalitions. Lucas leaves its specified record and background-law issues for remand. Espinosa does not turn procedural dissent into merits agreement. Benten decides the stay application on the remedy showing and leaves the constitutional claim unadjudicated. Canonical records control any conflict.

### Public procedure and open matters

Note §5 supplies participation, allotments and operative remedies. No supplied dated inventory event remains anticipated. Wyoming implementation, Coleman petition No. 91-8336 and Reynolds’s interim trials protection remain open on their recorded conditions. Harris’s further-stay condition remains personal to Harris. A remand to a lower court is not itself a new pending Supreme Court merits proceeding. No later external development or express next-term carry-forward has been supplied. The term is not closed by this Run.
